# MASTER FINDINGS REGISTER

Накопительный реестр EdgeHunter. Источники до Sprint 1 импортированы без удаления исходных ID: `EDGEHUNTER_DEFECT_REGISTRY.md` и `V2_DEFECT_REMEDIATION_REGISTER_2026-11-17.md`. Полные исходные evidence, статусы и обязательства сохранены в `MASTER_FINDINGS.json`; исходные реестры не изменены.

Записей: 1260 (D-series: 16; DEF-series: 34; Sprint 1: 7; дополнительно DATA/F/INF: 31; DOC claim reviews: 1160; full remediation: 5; source gaps: 1).

## Sprint 1

| ID | Status | Finding / root cause | Fix | Verification |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SPR1-DATA-001 | RESOLVED | Binance REST OI updated freshness before validating the response and sampled at local receive time; invalid OI could be treated as fresh. | Validate positive finite OI and exchange timestamp first; return (OI, exchange_ms), persist receive time separately, use exchange time for OI samples and freshness; validate seed history. | tests/test_regressions.py::test_open_interest_uses_exchange_timestamp_and_rejects_invalid_values |
| SPR1-DATA-002 | RESOLVED | HTTP 200 or parsed JSON was treated as a valid book and quality was refreshed without business-code or market-data checks. | Require HTTP success, OKX code 0 / Bybit retCode 0, bids and asks, finite positive levels, valid sorted non-crossed snapshot; expose transport/API/content/freshness stages and preserve prior fetched_at on invalid data. | tests/test_regressions.py::test_rest_book_validation_rejects_bad_structure_values_and_crossed_book; tests/test_regressions.py::test_rest_book_status_separates_stages_and_expires_freshness |
| SPR1-HEALTH-001 | RESOLVED | UI rendered any venue without connected=0/false as healthy; missing connection data became an implicit positive. | Add explicit CONNECTED, DISCONNECTED, NOT_CONFIGURED, STALE, UNKNOWN states; aggregation and UI treat absent data as UNKNOWN and unknown/stale/down as unhealthy. | tests/test_regressions.py::test_missing_websocket_connection_information_is_unknown; node --check web/app.js |
| SPR1-HEALTH-002 | RESOLVED | A configured stream with a known zero connection count and no prior messages remained UNKNOWN, conflating disconnection with missing information. | Known zero becomes DISCONNECTED; absent/invalid connection data remains UNKNOWN; grouped Binance streams use the same rules. | tests/test_regressions.py::test_configured_websocket_with_zero_connections_is_disconnected; tests/test_regressions.py::test_missing_websocket_connection_information_is_unknown; node --check web/app.js |
| SPR1-FLOW-001 | RESOLVED | REST 1m/5m taker aggregates were inserted into the per-second event-flow ring and represented as trade flow; spot delta combined candle aggregates and live prints without provenance. | Keep OHLCV history for warm-up; store legacy REST seed calls in a separately tagged observation buffer, exclude them from event-flow/CVD and whale inputs; expose separate spot candle delta, event-flow CVD and source label while retaining the legacy combined field. Candle divergence is documented as OHLCV-derived. | tests/test_regressions.py::test_rest_minute_seed_stays_out_of_event_flow_ring; targeted suite passed. |
| SPR1-FLOW-002 | RESOLVED | Activity was counted by non-zero venue net only; balanced buy/sell flow was inactive and top venue share was derived from absolute net, conflating participation and concentration. | Keep old fields and semantics; add gross_flow_5m, net_flow_5m, venues_flow_active, net_venues_active, top_gross_venue/share and net_top_venue_share; UI uses explicit gross concentration. | tests/test_regressions.py::test_cross_venue_gross_activity_survives_zero_net_flow |
| SPR1-MKT-001 | RESOLVED | Binance force orders could use original q when executed z was absent/zero, did not require FILLED, and cross-venue aggregate liquidations could suppress unrelated sweeps. Public feeds were described as complete. | Require FILLED, positive finite executed quantity z, positive average execution price ap and valid side/time; compute notional from z; correlate only same venue in the existing short interval; describe all public streams as partial observations. | tests/test_regressions.py::test_binance_liquidation_uses_filled_executed_quantity; tests/test_regressions.py::test_liquidation_attribution_can_be_limited_to_matching_venue; tests/test_regressions.py::test_other_venue_liquidations_do_not_suppress_sweep |

## Status history and interpretation

Все исходные ID и исходные формулировки сохранены. История импорта указана в JSON `status_history`; для Sprint 1 статус RESOLVED проставлен после целевых проверок. `DEF-MKT-004` оставлен PARTIAL: исправлены утверждения о полноте и source attribution, но отдельная количественная coverage/freshness модель для каждого liquidation stream остаётся открытой.

История исходного health finding сохранена; отдельный обратный дефект зарегистрирован как `SPR1-HEALTH-002`. Подробные функции, исправления, изменённые файлы и команды проверки находятся в `docs/SPRINT1_REPORT.md`.

## Additive source reconciliation (2026-11-19)

Этот раздел добавляет к прежнему реестру найденные источники DATA-001..002, F-01..14, INF-01..15 и DOCTRUTH ledger. Исходные ID не переименованы. Полные исходные evidence, excerpts и correction payloads хранятся в `MASTER_FINDINGS.json`; DOC записи являются claim-review inventory и сами по себе не утверждают наличие runtime-дефекта. Отсутствующие в доступном workspace семейства и независимые отчёты ChatGPT перечислены в `MASTER-SOURCE-001`; записи не синтезировались.

| DOC-0041 | IMPLEMENTATION_GAP | The briefing card as a picture, laid out as the owner's reference (an analyst's BingX card, 2026-10-05):    header   big price (green / red by the day), "<SYMBOL> · 1h · за сутки +x%", 24h high / low and the 1h ATR at the            right; the timeframe tabs 1м 15м 1ч 4ч 1д with 1ч selected   chart    ~3 days of 1h candles in the left ~60% of the width (room on the right), dates under it; six horizontal            zones - ГЛОБАЛЬНОЕ СОПРОТИВЛЕНИЕ / СОПРОТИВЛЕНИЕ / ЛОКАЛЬНОЕ СОПРОТИВЛЕНИЕ / ЗОНА ПОДДЕРЖКИ / РЕАКЦИЯ /            СЛЕДУЮЩАЯ ПОДДЕРЖКА / КЛЮЧЕВАЯ ПОДДЕРЖКА - each a tinted band with its name and role on the left, "lo – hi"            in the middle and a coloured tag at the right with its upper edge and the distance in %; the price as a            dashed line with a dark tag "price / HH:MM UTC"; the LONG tag (пробой и закрепление) near the top, the            SHORT tag (пробой поддержки) near the bottom   boxes    ЛОНГ (только после подтверждения): entry just above the watched zone after acceptance, targets at the three            resistances' upper edges, stop below the next support's upper edge, condition "пробой и ретест зоны X";            ШОРТ (при подтверждении): entry in the local resistance (отскок / реакция), targets the watched zone's low            and the supports' upper edges, stop above the second resistance, condition "сигнал на разворот в зоне"   footer   "СЕЙЧАС ЦЕНА ВНУТРИ ЗОНЫ lo – hi.; ЖДЁМ ПОДТВЕРЖДЕНИЯ." / "Входить в середине диапазона не рекомендуется." | DOC-0088 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Order books of the hot symbols, built the way Binance documents a local book ("How to manage a local order book correctly"): the <sym>@depth@500ms diff stream, buffered until a 1000-level REST snapshot arrives, events with u < lastUpdateId dropped, the first applied one spanning lastUpdateId, then every event's `pu` must equal the previous `u` (else the book is re-synced from a new snapshot).; Each event carries absolute quantities: 0 removes a level.; Within a minute of streaming the book reaches far beyond the 1000 levels of the snapshot (any level that changes is sent): 2026-10-05 the 500-level snapshots of the old design covered BTC only +-0.07% (less than one 0.1% heat bucket) and ETH +-0.2%.; COVERAGE (eng-80): the REST snapshot holds 1000 levels per side (BTC ~+-0.07%); beyond it the local book knows only the levels that CHANGED since the sync - a large order that just rests far from the price never appears, which is exactly what walls / bands look for.; So heat snapshots, walls and bands use only the price range the last snapshot covered (`cov_lo` ..; `cov_hi`), and a book whose mid left that range is re-synced (at most every 5 minutes) from a fresh snapshot (Binance's guidance: keep the book to the depth you actually hold).; Every decrease of a level is split into FILLED (taker trades at that price on that side, from the aggTrade stream, within ATTRIBUTION_MS) and PULLED (cancelled): liquidity that disappears without trades is the spoofing signature (~31% of large crypto orders could spoof, arXiv 2504.15908); liquidity that is traded into and stays / refills is absorption.; Additions are counted as ADDED.; Per heat bucket and minute: [added, pulled, filled] of each side.; Heatmap (as Bookmap draws it): a snapshot of the whole local book within +-heat_range_pct every heat_snapshot_sec, bucketed by heat_bucket_pct of price on a grid fixed per coin.; Walls: single levels >= wall_multiple x the median level notional within +-wall_range_pct that stood wall_min_age_sec (book depth refills within 5-10s after a trade - arXiv 1602.00731 - so a 10s-old order said little).; Liquidity bands: heat buckets that held heat_band_multiple x the snapshot's median bucket in >= heat_band_presence of the snapshots of heat_band_window_sec AND were not mostly pulled (pulled USD over the window < the band's average resting USD, or fills >= half of the pulls).; What the book is used for is decided elsewhere and measured: depth is context, sweep sizing and the slippage of an entry; bands / walls are not support or resistance by themselves (no study shows resting depth holds price on an hours horizon; order-flow imbalance explains price over seconds - Cont, Kukanov & Stoikov 2014). | depth.py:1 |

| DOC-0116 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Event risk from Binance's own public data (eng-53; Compass "7 phases" review: scheduled / announced events are the best-documented driver of dumps): - the Monitoring Tag (Binance's delisting watch list): the spot product list carries tags['Monitoring'] - live after   a tag, AVA -4.9%, SCR -7.5%, NFP -12%, WAN -34% within minutes / an hour (BeInCrypto, cryptorank); - a scheduled delisting of the perpetual: futures exchangeInfo sets the contract's deliveryDate to the removal time   (an ordinary perpetual carries 2100-12-25) - after Binance's delisting announcement of 2026-04-09: FUN -27.9%,   MDT -22.8%, FIO -20.5% within minutes. eng-135: exchange announcements, keyless, polled every 2 minutes (the tags / exchangeInfo stay hourly): - Binance CMS delisting catalogue (161): "Binance Will Delist A, B on <date>" = a spot delisting - longs vetoed until   the day after it (a futures delisting shows in deliveryDate above; Alpha / Margin removals are not a coin delisting); - Binance new listings (48): "Binance Will List X (X)" - journal only ('news'); - Upbit trade notices: "...; KRW 마켓 디지털 자산 추가" a KRW listing (journal only: listing effects fade in 1-2 days,   RockawayX / The TIE), "거래지원 종료" an Upbit delisting and "유의 종목 지정" its warning list - longs vetoed 14 days.; Upbit tickers can name another coin than Binance's (KRW-EDGE != EDGEUSDT): a false veto only costs a card.; Token unlocks (Keyrock: 90% of 16,000+ unlocks pressure the price for 30 days) need an external calendar: no free one (DefiLlama emissions answer 402, Tokenomist is a paid API).; A failed read keeps the last known state. | event_risk.py:1 |

| DOC-0122 | IMPLEMENTATION_GAP | Demo execution on BingX (virtual VST, `open-api-vst`): an eligible card that reaches ENTRY is sent there unless a configured demo gate (for example `demo_off_setups`, market/basis checks or an exchange constraint) rejects it.; Its result is recorded next to the journal's replay.; DEMO ONLY: sandbox mode is forced and cannot be switched off from the config.; Never an order on a real balance.  - ENTRY: a market order sized so the stop costs `risk_pct` of the demo balance (capped by `max_leverage`), then a   reduce-only stop-market at the card's SL and three reduce-only take-profits (thirds) at TP1 / TP2 / TP3. - The card finishes (SL / TP3 / CLOSED / INVALID / EXPIRED / MISSED after an entry): open orders are cancelled and   whatever is left of the position is closed at market. - Every minute the open demo trades are reconciled: a position the exchange closed (stop / last target) is finalized. - Each trade is a row of `demo_trades.db` (fills, fees, realized USD, R against the planned stop).; The API key is the owner's (config.local.toml, `[executor] api_key / api_secret`), a trade-only key.     python executor.py --check           markets, balance, position mode     python executor.py --test BTC/USDT:USDT    open and close the minimum size (checks order handling) | executor.py:1 |

| DOC-0185 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Public data beyond the streams (verified live 2026-09-30), all REST, light on bandwidth; context for the journal / stats / P(edge), never a trigger until the journal proves one:  - Deribit (no other source has options): the DVOL implied-volatility index of BTC / ETH (hourly candles) and the   put / call open-interest ratio of each one's options (get_book_summary_by_currency) -> market_state   `dvol_btc`, `dvol_chg_1h`, `pc_oi_btc`; a DVOL jump is logged as a market anomaly. - Hyperliquid `predictedFundings`: the NEXT funding of Binance, Bybit and Hyperliquid for every coin in one call ->   per coin `funding_spread` (max - min of the venues' 8h-equivalent rates): crowding on one venue. - OKX `rubik/stat/taker-volume` (5m taker buy / sell of each coin's contracts): the OKX flow windows are seeded at   start-up for the minutes before the streams (Binance's are seeded from its 1m klines). - eng-133 (shadow features, journaled, never a filter): Binance TradFi perpetuals (gold, WTI oil, SPY, QQQ) 1h / 24h   changes, the US cash-session flag, the previous day's CBOE VIX close, the Fear & Greed index and the spot-ETF net   flow (tools/farside_flows.py writes etf_flows.json; a day D is visible from D+1 08:00 UTC: no look-ahead). - Order books of OKX (`market/books`, 50 levels) and Bybit (`market/orderbook`, 50) for the hot coins every 60s ->   per coin `okx_book_imb` / `bybit_book_imb`: (bid - ask) / (bid + ask) within 0.5% of the mid (Binance has its own). - eng-134 (shadow, journaled, never a filter): Upbit's KRW tickers (all markets in one keyless call, every 60s) ->   per coin `kimchi_prem` % = the KRW price / Upbit's own KRW-USDT / the Binance futures price - 1 (no FX source   needed), computed at the card; NULL when the snapshot or the coin's last Upbit trade is older than 3 min, the coin   is not on Upbit, or \|premium\| > 30% (a ticker shared by two different coins). | extdata.py:1 |

| DOC-0314 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Iceberg detection: how a whale really accumulates (resting limit orders refilled at one price), not the aggressive market sweeps the old "whales" counted (measured x1.0: those were liquidations, stops and chasers).; For the hot coins with a live Binance book (depth20 @500ms): at one price level of one side, the volume EXECUTED against it (aggTrades whose taker hit that side at that price) exceeds `multiple` x the largest size ever VISIBLE there while the level held (no trade through it), and is at least `min_usd`.; A bid iceberg = a hidden buyer absorbing sells ("whale buying"), an ask iceberg the hidden seller.; Journaled as features (`iceberg_net_5m`: bid - ask iceberg USD over 5 minutes, `iceberg_n_5m`) and logged; measured before anything trades on it.; Sources: Bookmap's iceberg tracker, the usual definition (executed > displayed at a level that refills). | iceberg.py:1 |
| DOC-0315 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Bids and asks are kept apart so a trade only touches its own side: scanning every tracked level on every aggTrade     stalled the event loop on ETH / BTC (live 2026-10-01, watchdog: iceberg.on_trade).; A level must REFILL - be shown     again in >= `min_refills` book snapshots after trades hit it - before it counts: depth20 arrives every 500 ms, so     "executed > the largest size ever shown" alone fired on every busy ETH level where new orders simply arrived     between two snapshots (112 of 157 events on ETH, "$61k executed vs $212 shown"). | iceberg.py:40 |
| DOC-0316 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Largest size seen at each price of the live book (USD); a level traded against and shown again = a refill. | iceberg.py:62 |

| DOC-0323 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Core Tier-0 raw impulse metrics from in-memory stream data (no REST, no Polars); the order-flow columns are     filled in by the scanner from FlowStore / WhaleBook.      rvol_1m: quote volume of the last 60 seconds from the futures 1m kline stream (the current minute plus the     not-yet-elapsed share of the previous one) divided by the average minute of the last 24h.     whale_net_5m: net whale flow over the window in units of the symbol's whale size (legacy input: whale_trades     as (ts_ms, signed usd, ...) records; the scanner overrides it with WhaleBook.net_units).     price_z / oi_z: the 5m change divided by the coin's typical 5m change (15m std / sqrt(3)); 0 when unknown. | indicators.py:229 |
| DOC-0324 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | The coin's intraday periodicity from ~4 weeks of 1h candles [ts, o, h, l, c, quote_vol, ...]: per UTC hour the     median quote volume and the median \|1h return\| of weekdays, each divided by its mean over the 24 hours, and the     weekend / weekday ratio of both.; Relative volume and volatility then compare a minute with the same time of day     ("relative volume at time"; Andersen-Bollerslev periodicity): against a flat 24h average, the US open produced     false anomalies and quiet hours / weekends hid real ones (Boudt, Croux & Laurent 2011; crypto: arXiv 2109.12142). | indicators.py:310 |

| DOC-0417 | UNVERIFIED | Where the bot's memory sits, cheap enough for the live loop (tracemalloc / objgraph walk every object and froze it: 2026-10-05, 72s stalls at 1.8 GB).  - `process_breakdown()`: this process's committed memory by region class (Windows VirtualQuery, the VMMap view):   pymalloc arenas (1 MB blocks = Python objects), large private blocks (numpy / polars buffers, allocator segments),   images (DLL code). tools/vmmap.py prints the same for another process from outside. - `structures(fetcher, scanner)`: sizes of the bot's own containers, polars `estimated_size`, numpy `nbytes`, Python   containers estimated from a sample of their elements; `MarketData` fields summed over all coins. | memreport.py:1 |

| DOC-0420 | UNVERIFIED | MB by class: private, arenas (pymalloc 1 MB blocks), small (< 1 MB), mid (1-8 MB), large (>= 8 MB), image. | memreport.py:97 |
| DOC-0421 | UNVERIFIED | The same view from /proc/<pid>/smaps (0 = this process): MB resident + swapped by class.; `private` = anonymous     writable memory, split into `heap` (brk), `small` (< 1 MB), `mid` (1-8 MB), `large` (>= 8 MB) by the size of the     mapping; `swap` = the part of it that is swapped out; `image` = file-backed mappings.; The kernel merges adjacent     anonymous mappings, so a block of several allocator segments reads as one `large`; pymalloc arenas cannot be told     apart (no `arenas` key). | memreport.py:119 |

| DOC-0425 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Machine learning that adapts to the current market instead of fixed thresholds fitted to the past.; 1.; AnomalyModel (scikit-learn IsolationForest): Tier-0 anomalies as an UNUSUAL COMBINATION of a coin's live    features (RVOL, price / OI z-scores, whale sweeps, liquidations, flow intensity, one-venue share, algo    clusters), refitted every hour on the last hours of the whole universe.; It replaces the OR of seven fixed    thresholds (which flagged ~1000 onsets an hour, most of them noise) once it has been fitted; until then the    rules decide.; No labels needed.; 2.; RegimeModel (scikit-learn GaussianMixture): the market regime from market-wide features of every closed 15m    candle (BTC 1h move and volatility, breadth, median funding / OI change / RVOL).; Clusters are named from their    centers ('up·volatile', 'flat·quiet', ...).; Every journal row carries the regime; stats.py splits Edge by it    and journal.veto_reason mutes a setup whose Edge in the current regime is proven negative.; 3.; SuccessModel (River when available; a small compatibility model on Python 3.10): online logistic regression +    drift detection for P(4h Edge > 0) of a directional signal from journal features.; It learns from every labelled row    as it comes in (and replays the journal after a restart), and starts over when error rates drift.; Shadow mode: stored in the journal and shown on cards once trained, never used as a filter until stats.py    proves it separates winners from losers. | ml.py:1 |

| DOC-0473 | CONFIRMED_LIMITED | eng-168t (audit #6): the macro releases from their OFFICIAL schedules - the primary source of the entry blackout and of the briefing's macro line; ForexFactory's weekly feed (macro_calendar.LIVE) stays the fallback / cross-check.; Sources (each fetched with a neutral User-Agent, cached in models/official_calendar.json): - BLS iCalendar (https://www.bls.gov/schedule/news_release/bls.ics): CPI, Employment Situation (NFP), PPI; times in   the feed's own zone (TZID=US-Eastern -> America/New_York); - ISM release calendar (rob-report-calendar): the year's table of Manufacturing / Services PMI release days (holiday   moves included), 10:00 a.m.; ET; - FOMC calendar (fomccalendars.htm): meeting dates; the policy statement at 2:00 p.m.; ET on the meeting's last day and   the press conference at 2:30 p.m.; ET (two events, the statement is the blackout's anchor).; Notation votes skipped.; Every event: {id, type, source, t (UTC ms), tz, title, status}. status 'scheduled' or 'unconfirmed' - an event a previous fetch listed in the future and the new fetch no longer lists is KEPT as 'unconfirmed' (a partial response is not a cancellation; a reschedule then blocks both times, the conservative side).; Coverage (completeness, not just reachability): a category is covered when its source was fetched successfully within OFFICIAL_MAX_AGE_SEC AND that source's schedule reaches at least COVER_DAYS ahead (an empty / short schedule - ISM before it posts next year's table - is NOT 'no events').; Uncovered categories fall back to the fresh ForexFactory week; with neither, macro_calendar.live_ok() is False and new entries wait (fail-closed, eng-168s). | official_calendar.py:1 |

| DOC-0494 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Pretraining on the public Binance archive (history.py) BEFORE the bot starts; live, the models keep learning.; What is trained (everything the archive has the inputs for): - the market regime model (ml.RegimeModel): the same market-wide features per closed 15m candle (BTC 1h move and   volatility, breadth, median funding / 1h OI change / RVOL) over months of history instead of the last days; - P(edge) (ml.SuccessModel): the journal rows the bot would have written - phase setups (A-F) scored by the same   indicator / scorer code on 15m candles built from 1m klines (futures + spot, OI from the 5-minute metrics, funding   from the funding history), liquidity sweeps and breakouts from the same detector (strategies.structure.build_context   on each closed candle with its 8 days of 1h candles), Tier-0 anomalies / impulses from the same   calculate_tier0_metrics + rules on every minute that could qualify - each with its context (market / BTC moves, the   coin's 1h trend, volatility, 24h volume, long-short ratios, regime, market crowd), the journal's cooldown, and the   label the live journal gives it (the 4h return in the signal's direction beat the median of every coin).; Not in the archive (the models learn them live only): liquidations, order books, the other venues' flows / OI, Hyperliquid accounts, Coinbase, the IsolationForest verdict (Tier-0 rows here are the rule-based ones).; Whale sizes come from live sweeps (kept across restarts).; The IsolationForest itself is not pretrained: it judges "unusual compared with the last 3 hours", which history cannot tell (its window survives restarts, ml_state.pkl).; Run:  .venv\Scripts\python pretrain.py [--months 6] [--min-volume 3e6] [--workers 3] [--rate 1000000] Downloads are cached (history_cache/); a re-run only computes.; The result is pretrained.pkl; the bot loads it at start (journal P(edge), scanner regimes) and keeps learning on top of it.; Run it while the bot is stopped or with a low --rate: the download shares the link with the live streams. | pretrain.py:1 |

| DOC-0512 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Live pump / dump monitor of small caps (the rules of `research_pumps.py`, on each coin's closed 1h candles).; A PUMP: the close >= 15% above the lowest low of the last 6 hours, the hours since that low traded >= 3x the average hour of the week before (DUMP: the mirror).; Small caps only (not among the 30 most traded coins by 24h volume).; After the event the monitor follows the first pullback / bounce: the 38.2-61.8% zone of the move, the trigger (a 1h close beyond the previous candle's extreme after the zone was touched), stop beyond the pullback's extreme (+ 0.25 ATR1h), TP1 = the move's extreme, TP2 = its 1.618 extension.; Measured (research/pumps.md, research/pumps_holdout.md; hit rate vs the random-walk rate of the same trade): - after a DUMP, short the first bounce: 2023-10..2026-08 +0.17R / +0.16R (older / newer half), the untouched   2021-04..2023-09 +0.36R / +0.34R, TP1 hit 62-69% vs 45-57% geometry: the one pre-registered part that held; - after a PUMP, buy the first pullback: +0.13R / +0.14R in 2023-26 but -0.04R / +0.13R in 2021-23 with the hit   rate AT the geometry: not confirmed.; Information, never an order; samples are small (a few hundred trades per period). | pumps.py:1 |

| DOC-0517 | UNVERIFIED | Proactive REST rate limiting.; Every REST call goes through a RestGate.; A gate does two things: - it stays under a weight budget instead of waiting for a ban: after each call it reads the exchange's own counter   (Binance `X-MBX-USED-WEIGHT-1M`, Bybit `X-Bapi-Limit-Status`), keeps a local estimate in between, and makes   callers wait for the next minute when the budget for their priority is used up (priority 0 may use the whole   budget, lower priorities a smaller share, so candle backfills never queue behind journal evaluation); - on a 429 / 418 / 403 it pauses every caller of the gate (and of gates sharing its BanState) until the ban ends,   parsed from `banned until <ms>` or `Retry-After`, with exponential backoff as a fallback. | ratelimit.py:1 |
| DOC-0518 | UNVERIFIED | Shared pause: one ban (per IP / API cluster) stops every gate that shares this object. | ratelimit.py:31 |
| DOC-0519 | UNVERIFIED | Run `exchange.<method>(*args, **kwargs)` within the budget; on 429/418 pause everyone and retry. | ratelimit.py:130 |
| DOC-0520 | UNVERIFIED | Optional append-only raw event journal for independent validation.; The logger is disabled unless ``EDGEHUNTER_RAW_LOG_DIR`` is set.; It stores one gzip JSONL member per event, preserving the bytes received before parsing and the local receive clocks.; It deliberately has no dependency on the scanner's signal or replay code. | raw_event_log.py:1 |
| DOC-0521 | UNVERIFIED | Wait for queued records (offline readers / orderly shutdown; never on the event loop). | raw_event_log.py:80 |
| DOC-0522 | UNVERIFIED | Record a REST response using the same append-only envelope. | raw_event_log.py:96 |
| DOC-0523 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | Data recorder: the live-only inputs the Binance public archive does not have, written to Parquet so they can be tested offline later (as filters for 1h-4h trades, never as tick strategies: a manual entry is seconds late).  - flow:  per second x venue x coin, taker buy / sell USD, long / short liquidation USD, trade count (all six venues;          the FlowStore ring buffers, so nothing new is streamed).; Every Binance / Bybit / OKX liquidation event is          already kept one by one in the journal's `liquidations` table. - depth: once a minute, the latest 30s snapshot (CONFIG.depth.heat_snapshot_sec) of each hot coin's local book (kept          from the diff stream; REST only on (re)sync, so Binance weight does not limit this): best bid / ask, and USD          resting in 0.1% bands from the mid out to 5% per side (flat columns bid_00..bid_49 / ask_00..ask_49), plus          the live metrics (spread, band depth / imbalance, persistent walls). - walls: eng-170, every wall that ended (young ones too, unlike the dashboard's >= 30s list): first seen / ended          (30s snapshot resolution), side, price, peak USD, multiple of the median level, filled / pulled USD, why. - deriv: per minute x venue x coin, OI (coins and USD), mark, 8h funding (Bybit / OKX / Bitget / Hyperliquid OI is          not in any public archive).; Layout: <root>/<kind>/date=YYYY-MM-DD/HHMM.parquet per minute, merged into HH.parquet once the hour is over.; The event loop only copies the finished minute out of the ring buffers (numpy slices, ~1 ms) and hands it to the bot's worker PROCESS (the level-map pool); building frames and writing Parquet happen there, so disk I/O and polars never hold the loop's GIL. | recorder.py:1 |

| DOC-0632 | MISATTRIBUTED | Asymmetrical scoring of five market phases on CLOSED 15m candles, vectorized with pl.when().then().otherwise().; Each condition adds points independently; long = A + B + E (bullish), short = C + D (bearish).; The conditions follow the standard crypto order-flow reading:     - price/OI matrix: price up + OI up = new longs (strong), price up + OI down = short covering (fades),       price down + OI up = new shorts (strong), price down + OI down = long liquidation (exhaustion, near bottoms);     - CVD: net taker selling absorbed while price is flat = accumulation, a new high on net taker selling =       bearish divergence (distribution); spot-led flow is sturdier than perp-led flow;     - funding: extreme positive = crowded longs, extreme negative = crowded shorts.; The weights are hand-set hypotheses; the journal (stats.py) measures them live.; All frames carry a 'symbol' column (see indicators.compute_indicators_batch), so one call scores every symbol. | scorer.py:25 |
| DOC-0633 | UNVERIFIED | Score every symbol in one pass and return, per symbol, the scored row of its last CLOSED candle     (timestamp <= closed_until[symbol], the base market's newest finalized candle).; Structured phases are read     only from closed candles: an in-progress 15m candle can still change its close, wick and delta. | scorer.py:493 |
| DOC-0634 | UNVERIFIED | The per-scan light pass: cached closed-candle rows (structured scores) + fresh Tier-0 metrics -> signals.; Symbols without candle history still get the Tier-0 check. | scorer.py:517 |
| DOC-0635 | UNVERIFIED | Heavy + light pass in one call (tools and tests).; Without closed_until every symbol's last row counts. | scorer.py:548 |

| DOC-0637 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Spot vs perp net flow over the last hour.; Perp-led: the perp side drives the move while spot disagrees or         barely participates (spot net flow < perp_led_spot_share of the perp net flow); such moves fade more often.; The perp side uses the multi-venue aggregate once enough candles were accumulated (agg_ready). | scorer.py:120 |
| DOC-0638 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Tier-0: raw impulse, independent of Score and phases.; Any single condition is enough:         - RVOL_1m above the threshold on a minute of real size, except in the first seconds after a quarter-hour           boundary (algo bursts);         - \|price 5m\| / \|OI 5m\| above BOTH the absolute floor and the coin's own z-score threshold;         - net whale sweeps >= whale_net_units; an algo cluster; a liquidation cascade;         - ONE-VENUE: most of the 5m net flow came from a single venue while several are active. | scorer.py:142 |
| DOC-0639 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | OI rising over the last hour in the coin's own terms (z >= trend_oi_z of its 1h change, floor), else fixed. | scorer.py:196 |
| DOC-0640 | MISATTRIBUTED | Phase A: accumulation (bullish) - a tight range where aggressive selling is absorbed by resting bids.         - Bollinger width near its lookback minimum (squeeze) (+1)         - Range over the window within flat_range_atr ATRs while taker SELLERS dominate its volume: the sells are           absorbed, price does not break down (+2)         - Futures OI growing over the same window while the range holds: positions built quietly (+1) | scorer.py:207 |
| DOC-0641 | MISATTRIBUTED | Phase B: Wyckoff spring (bullish) - a shallow sweep below an established trading range that sellers could         not hold.         - The prior 20 candles form a range (high - low within spring_range_atr ATRs) that drifted sideways (net move           <= spring_max_drift of that span), not a downtrend channel         - The low pierces that range's low by at most spring_max_depth_atr ATRs and the candle closes back above it         - Close in the top 30% of the candle on net taker selling: the sell stops were absorbed by bids         Volume is not required: a quiet spring is the classic strong one; the LTF entry requires the Wyckoff "test"         (a pullback on lower volume, levels.pullback_volume_ok). | scorer.py:230 |
| DOC-0642 | MISATTRIBUTED | Phase C: distribution / bull trap (bearish), all at a new 50-candle high.         - Net taker flow over the last 20 candles clearly negative (buyers <= 47.5%): price up on selling =           bearish CVD divergence, someone sells into strength (+2)         - Funding above the extreme: longs pay heavily at the top (+1)         - OI still rising with funding crowded (> orderflow.crowded_funding_long): leveraged longs piling in late (+1)         - Spot net selling over the last hour at the high: the real (unleveraged) side distributes (+1) | scorer.py:260 |
| DOC-0643 | MISATTRIBUTED | Phase D: breakdown continuation (bearish) - price down + OI up = new shorts with conviction.         - Close below the lower Bollinger band out of a squeeze (Bollinger's method I)         - Taker sells dominate the candle (delta_ratio < dump_delta_ratio)         - OI up at least dump_oi_min over the short window (new shorts opening, not just longs closing)         - Funding not already crowded short (squeeze risk)         Falling price with collapsing OI is long liquidation, i.e. exhaustion; that belongs to phase E.; A perp-led breakdown (spot not selling) is vetoed downstream (journal.veto_reason). | scorer.py:289 |
| DOC-0644 | MISATTRIBUTED | Phase E: capitulation / selling climax (bullish reversal candidate).         - Volume climax (RVOL > rvol_anomaly_threshold) after a real decline (>= capitulation_min_drop over the           window), with OI dropping sharply over the short window: forced long liquidation (+2)         - Funding below the negative extreme: shorts crowded after the flush (+1)         - Reversal confirmation: long lower wick or buyers >= 55% of the candle (+2)         - Spot net buying over the last hour: spot absorbs the perp flush (+1)         - Long liquidations cascading (observed, not inferred from OI) (+1) | scorer.py:311 |
| DOC-0645 | MISATTRIBUTED | Phase F: markup breakout continuation (bullish), the mirror of phase D - price up + OI up = new longs with         conviction.         - Close above the upper Bollinger band out of a squeeze (Bollinger's method I: the band break counts only after           the bands narrowed to their lookback minimum; a tag of a wide band is not a signal)         - Taker buys dominate the candle (delta_ratio > markup_delta_ratio)         - OI up at least dump_oi_min over the short window (new longs opening, not just shorts covering)         - Funding not already at the long extreme (a crowded top)         Rising price with collapsing OI is a short squeeze, which fades; a perp-led breakout (spot not buying) is         vetoed downstream (journal.veto_reason). | scorer.py:350 |

| DOC-0647 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | False acceptance of the published briefing zones (eng-168z1, audit #9) - a DESCRIPTIVE measurement of the zones the briefings actually showed (frozen in data/briefings.jsonl), not of today's map and not a backtest of new rules.; Per published record (silent ones excluded) and zone: the first CONFIRMING close after the post - a close above the zone's hi (up) or below its lo (down) on the confirmation timeframe - when price was NOT already beyond that edge at the post.; Counted twice, independently, on 1h and on 4h closes (the paired report), and once on the zone's own conf_tf when the record has it (the policy report).; After the confirming close, on the 1h closes that follow (the clock starts at the confirming candle's CLOSE, for 1h and 4h alike), within N = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 / 12 hours:   inside  - a 1h close back between lo and hi;   origin  - a 1h close back beyond the opposite edge (the side the move came from);   retouch - a 1h candle's range back into the band (wick, the close may stay beyond).; F_N = events with that return within N h / confirmations with the full N h observed (younger ones are left out of the denominator, not counted as held).; One breakout of one zone posted in several briefings counts once (key: coin, zone edges rounded to 0.1 ATR, direction, confirming candle).; Zone type: the timeframes of its pivots from the saved 'tfs' (new records) or parsed from its description '(1h/4h)'.; PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python scripts/acceptance_briefing.py [days=16] | scripts/acceptance_briefing.py:1 |

| DOC-0649 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | Trace of the entered briefing scenarios (eng-168v, audit #7): per scenario the real geometry after the reaction, the excursions BEFORE the first stop vs over the whole 72h, the first outcome and its time, and the obstacles between the entry and TP1 that existed at the post: - ladder: the zones the briefing itself published (record['zones']) - by construction none should lie in between; - raw:    the sr.zones clusters rebuilt from Binance candles closed BEFORE the post (the same call key_zones makes,           before its span filter, merge and ladder) - a raw zone in between = an obstacle the ladder dropped or merged; - pdhl:   the previous day / week high / low from the closed daily candles (the map's PDH / PDL / PWH / PWL; the other           map levels - FVG / OB / POC - cannot be rebuilt offline and are not counted).; Writes data/trace_briefing.csv and prints a summary plus the N worst traces.; A measurement of the rules as they ran.; PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python scripts/trace_briefing.py [days=16] [traces=5] | scripts/trace_briefing.py:1 |

| DOC-0657 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Signal lifecycle and anomaly memory for the dashboard.; A directional setup belongs to a strategy (strategies/): the strategy turns it into a PLAN (its anchor - a leg, a level or a range -, entry style, zone, invalidation, stop reference and targets by intent); this module runs the same lifecycle for every plan:      BIAS    the coin's history / level map is still loading (at most plan_wait_sec for 1h-dependent strategies), or             the plan stopped existing later; a setup without an anchor never becomes a card (vetoed 'no_leg')     WATCH   waiting for price to reach the plan's zone     ZONE    price is in the zone: waiting for the structure-shift trigger on the plan's trigger timeframe     ENTRY   entry / SL / TP set: at the confirmation (plan entry 'confirm') when R:R to TP1 allows it, else on the             trigger in the zone     TP1/TP2 targets reached, trade still managed;  final: TP3, SL, CLOSED (time / opposite setup), INVALID, MISSED,             EXPIRED  Shared rules: INVALID beyond the plan's invalidation (fixed at the card's start when the extreme was known at the confirmation); MISSED when TP1 (fixed beyond the furthest price reached since the anchor) is reached before an entry; a continuation whose leg makes a new extreme goes back to waiting and is re-measured; a new card closes the open card of the opposite direction when its structure timeframe is the same or higher; a card that finished blocks a new one on the same anchor; every setup carries its market crowd (CONFIG.market) and context (trend, volatility) for the journal / stats / ML - no limit on the number of cards.; Filters (each journaled with its reason instead of silently dropped, so stats.py can measure it): - a perp-led continuation (spot disagrees) never becomes a card (journal.veto_reason); - a strategy's context policy (a sweep against the coin's 1h trend: 'against_trend'), a disabled strategy; - a spring's entry needs its "test": the pullback trades on lower volume than the spring candle; - every entry carries its confirmations (strategies/confirm.py: taker flow, spot flow, OI, order flow at the   extreme); a required one that is negative vetoes it ('no_oi', 'no_orderflow', ...: journaled as a vetoed entry,   the card keeps waiting for the next trigger).; Statuses follow the futures price at each scan; the journal replays each entry on 1m candles with a manual fill.; Partial phases go to the watchlist; other Tier-0 anomalies are remembered for CONFIG.tier0.display_hold_sec. | signal_book.py:1 |

| DOC-0715 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | eng-158: live 1m watch of the small caps outside the universe for the start of pumps and dumps.; The universe holds ~70 data-complete coins; the biggest pumps / dumps happen in the coins outside it (no spot pair, fresh listings, < 2 other venues).; Until now those were read once an hour on 1h candles and journaled only (Scanner.wide_watch_loop), so a -45% dump (KAT, WET 24.10) was seen an hour late and never traded.; Here every such perpetual (wide_min_volume, the wide watch's list) gets its Binance futures 1m kline stream on ONE dynamic connection (no restart of the universe's streams when the list changes - eng-102).; Per coin only a ring of the last `smallcap_minutes` final minutes is kept (tuples, ~7 MB for 150 coins).; On each final minute a cheap gate (\|15m move\| >= pump_start_min_pct) runs first; only then the coin's 15m volatility and its OI change are fetched by REST and PUMP START / DUMP START (strategies.pump) decide.; A hit becomes an ordinary setup signal for the SignalBook: the card, the journal and the demo trade follow the same rules as in the universe.; The coin's minutes / price feed the card through Scanner._minute_candles / _price_of while it is not in the universe.; Inputs that differ from the universe (no spot pair, no order books here): - spot taker flow -> the FUTURES taker flow of the last 5 minutes (sig 'flow_src' = 'futures': journaled); - rvol_1m -> the last minute's quote volume / (24h quote volume / 1440), the universe's own definition; - the plan's ATR -> the 1h ATR(14) of the wide watch's 1h candles / 2 (a 15m ATR by the square-root rule). | smallcap.py:1 |

| DOC-0722 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | Support / resistance from where price actually turned (eng-75, the owner: "you don't understand what support and resistance are and what forms them"; web-checked 2026-10-08: Fidelity, Investing.com, Capital.com, Dukascopy, Wealthsimple, FTMO, Traders Agency).; What forms a level, by the sources: - price reversed there: a swing high / low is where supply and demand changed hands (old highs resist, old lows   support); - memory: traders trapped at that price exit at breakeven when it returns, those who missed it buy / sell there -   hence ROLE REVERSAL: a broken support resists from below, a broken resistance supports from above; - strength: how many times price reacted there, how big the reaction was (size and duration), the volume on the   touches, the timeframe (4h / daily / weekly outweigh 1h) and recency; touches alone are not enough (FTMO: a level   tested too often may be weaker - orders rest behind it), the quality of the reaction counts.; So a zone here is a CLUSTER OF SWING PIVOTS of the 1h / 4h / daily candles, not a catalogue of constructs: - pivots: fractal swings (the candle's high above `width` neighbours on both sides; lows mirrored), each with its   reaction (the largest move away in the next `react_bars` candles, in that timeframe's ATR) and its volume   (the candle's volume / the median of the 20 before); - pivots within `tol` of each other (by price) form one zone, its band = the pivots' wicks (at most `max_width`); - touches = pivots + the later 1h candles that wicked into the band and closed back out on the side they came   from (rejections); flip = the cluster holds both swing highs and swing lows (the level changed role); - strength = sum over the pivots of tf weight x (1 + reaction, capped) x volume factor x recency decay, plus half a   point per rejection.; The weights are hypotheses - research_sr.py measures whether these zones hold price better   than placebo prices before anything trades on them. | sr.py:1 |

| DOC-0725 | UNVERIFIED | eng-168n: how price met the band [lo, hi] on the closed 1h candles AFTER its first pivot, the pivots' own bars     (within one candle of their timeframe) left out.; An approach starts on a candle that came from outside (the     previous close beyond one edge) and traded into the band; it ends on the first close outside: back on the     approach side = a rejection, beyond the far edge = a break (closes inside keep it open). max_pierce_atr: the deepest     wick past the near edge on a rejection (max_pierce_on_rejection), in ATR14(1h). | sr.py:138 |
| DOC-0726 | UNVERIFIED | Statistics over the signal journal (signals.db): what the price did after each kind of signal.      python stats.py                 # rows of the current signal logic (CONFIG.journal.logic_version)     python stats.py --all-versions  # every row, including older logic     python stats.py --days 7        # last 7 days     python stats.py --last 30       # also list the 30 most recent signals with their outcomes  Returns are measured from the futures last price at the signal, in the signal's direction: for BEAR / IMPULSE DOWN a falling price counts as a gain. "Net" figures subtract the round-trip fee (CONFIG.journal.fee_round_trip).; Market baseline: the MEAN return of the WHOLE universe over the same window (not the median: coins beat the median by ~0.11% per 4h on average - right-skewed returns - which made every long look good and every short bad), from the price snapshots the scanner stores every 15 minutes (older databases without snapshots fall back to the mean of the coins logged in the same hour). "Edge" is a setup's return in its direction minus that baseline, so a falling market doesn't make every short look good. [lo..hi] = 90% bootstrap interval of the mean: an interval that straddles 0 means the sample cannot tell the setup from noise yet. | stats.py:1 |

| DOC-0755 | UNVERIFIED | Harvey & Liu (2015, "Backtesting", JPM): the Sharpe haircut for multiple testing - the t-stat of the per-cluster     Sharpe (t = SR x sqrt(n)), its two-sided p-value Bonferroni-adjusted for `trials` (p x M, their most conservative     rule), translated back to a Sharpe; haircut = 1 - SR_adj / SR (100% = nothing left once the trials are counted).; Bonferroni is the strictest of their three (Bonferroni / Holm / BHY; they advocate BHY, which needs every trial's     p-value - we have only the live cells'): an UPPER bound of the haircut. | stats.py:980 |
| DOC-0756 | UNVERIFIED | Bailey, Borwein, Lopez de Prado & Zhu (2017): the probability of backtest overfitting by combinatorially symmetric     cross-validation.; `matrix` = T periods x N configurations (here: days x strategies, the day's summed R).; The T rows     are cut into `blocks` contiguous parts; for every half / half split the in-sample best configuration's     out-of-sample relative rank w gives logit ln(w / (1 - w)); PBO = the share of splits with logit <= 0 (the IS winner     at or below the OOS median). ~0.5 = choosing the best strategy is a coin flip. | stats.py:999 |

| DOC-0773 | UNVERIFIED | The strategy interface.; A strategy turns a setup (a detected event or a scored phase) into a PLAN, a dict:      strategy, direction, anchor {type 'leg' \| 'level' \| 'range', tf, text, ...}     tf, trigger_minutes          structure timeframe and the entry trigger's (3-6x finer)     atr, recent_atr, leg_atr     15m ATR14, the last hour's 15m ATR (stop floor), the anchor's own ATR (stop buffer)     entry                        'confirm' (enter at the confirmation, else wait in the zone) \| 'pullback'     zone_lo, zone_hi, zone_src   where the trigger is looked for once price is there     invalid                      price beyond which the idea is wrong (None: none before the entry)     fix_invalid                  the invalid price is fixed when the card starts (an extreme known at the confirmation)     reset                        continuation: price beyond it = the leg extended -> back to waiting, re-measured     missable                     MISSED when TP1 is reached before an entry     stop_ref / stop_mid          the stop goes beyond stop_ref (and the trigger's swing) or sits at stop_mid     targets, target_src          fixed at the card's start beyond `reached` (retarget: re-measured from the entry)     intent, fillers, fill_label  target intent and the anchor's own fillers (fib / measured moves)     zones                        merged level map (stop pools, targets)     key                          the anchor's identity: a finished card blocks a new one on the same anchor     impulse                      the leg (leg anchors) for the retracement read of the zone  Lifecycle and journal transport are shared; trade decisions belong to each playbook contract and are dispatched by the owning Strategy instance. | strategies/base.py:1 |

| DOC-0785 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Top-down bias (HTF -> MTF -> LTF), the common ground of the sources (2026-10-05, the owner's rule "the higher timeframe always comes first"; web-checked): - Elder's triple screen: the long-term tide decides the side, the intermediate wave is a pullback AGAINST it (buy   declines in an up tide), the short-term ripple times the entry; timeframes x5 apart. - ICT top-down: monthly / weekly / daily / 4h give one bias - bullish = longs only, bearish = shorts only, neutral = no   trade; an LTF setup against the HTF bias "is a trap, not a trade". - Weinstein / Shannon: the higher timeframe's stage (base, markup, top, markdown) and its moving averages - longs in   markup only. - Time-series momentum (Moskowitz, Ooi & Pedersen 2012; crypto practice blends 1 / 3 / 12 months): the academic part. - Ranges: mean reversion only inside a higher-timeframe range, from its edges, out at its middle.; State of one coin from its own candles (the last CLOSED ones): - tsm: the sign mean of the 30 / 90 / 365-day returns (-1 .. +1), the momentum blend; - w1: the weekly close vs the EMA20 of the weekly closes (+1 / -1); - d1 / h4: +1 when the close is above the EMA50 and the EMA50 rose over 5 days / 6 candles, -1 mirrored, 0 else; - adx4: ADX(14) of the 4h candles.; Bias: - 'bull': tsm > 0 and the tide up - the daily trend for intraday methods (the 4h is their wave: a pullback against   the tide is Elder's entry, not a veto), the weekly for daily methods (Connors, cup: the daily is their wave); - 'bear': mirrored; - 'range': d1 = 0 and adx4 < 25 (no daily trend, a non-trending 4h); - 'conflict': anything else (the timeframes disagree: no trade - ICT's neutral). | strategies/bias.py:1 |

| DOC-0795 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Liquidation exhaustion (FLUSH REVERSAL / SQUEEZE REVERSAL) - eng-127: SHADOW only ('cascade_shadow'): "buy after a cascade" is trader folklore (no tested rules); the adjacent evidence is intraday reversal after large price jumps (Wen, Bouri, Xu, Zhao 2022) and overreaction after negative Bitcoin shocks.; A cascade of forced liquidations overshoots mechanically and price snaps back once the forced flow stops; the entry needs the extreme move AND a sign the flush is over.; Wyckoff (Pruden, Weis): a liquidation cascade is a selling / buying climax; the climax itself is not bought - the trade comes on the SECONDARY TEST, a return toward the climax's extreme on lower volume (eng-30: the entry trigger needs the pullback's average minute <= spring_test_volume_ratio x the climax's, like a spring's test).  eng-58: a selling climax ENDS a decline (Wyckoff's phase A): the close 24 1h candles before the cascade must be beyond its start in the cascade's direction (above for a flush of longs), else 'no_prior_trend'; the structure is 15m (our timeframe rule), the entry 5m.; Anchor: a real leg - the cascade, from the extreme of the exhaustion_pre_minutes before it to its extreme.; The extreme keeps following price until the entry (nobody knows where a running cascade ends), so there is no fixed invalidation; the card waits near the extreme for a 5m structure shift against the cascade.; Targets: the mean (VWAP anchored at the cascade's start, the map's VWAP / POC / nodes), the cascade's 0.382 / 0.5 / 0.618 retracements fill empty slots. | strategies/cascade.py:1 |

| DOC-0801 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Classic published methods added as their authors define them (eng-29), on the session instruments only (CONFIG.strategies.orb_bases: the majors, gold, silver, oil):  - value_area  Dalton's 80% rule by SESSION (eng-95; Dalton "Mind Over Markets"): the session (Asia / London / New York,               weekdays) opens outside the previous same session's TPO value area (70% of time at price, eng-58),               closes two consecutive 30-minute periods back inside it -> entry at the second period's close, target               the far edge, out at the session's close (session_va_event, VA80).; Beside it the failed auction               (eng-127, failed_auction_event, AUCTION LONG / SHORT).; Both run in the maps worker at 15m closes. - orb         Toby Crabel's opening range breakout ("Playing the Opening Range Breakout", Stocks & Commodities): the               range of a session's first 5 minutes, stop orders the Stretch (10-day SMA of min(High - Open,               Open - Low)) beyond its high / low; the first level traded through is the entry (ON THE TOUCH, a stop               order), the other level the stop; out at the session's close (eng-146; eng-145's 15m               Parabolic SAR exit reverted).; Checked               loop-side on every final minute (session_orb via strategies.loopside.LoopDetectors). - connors     Larry Connors' RSI(2) on DAILY candles, with the trend (close above / below the 200-day SMA): RSI2X = his               original rule (a close with RSI(2) < 5, buy on the close; the mirrored short is shadow), RSI2 = the               cross-back (eng-84: RSI(2) comes back out of the extreme, the close still beyond the SMA5).; Out on the               first daily close beyond the current SMA5.; He uses no stop: the R unit is 1 daily ATR, position at 1x,               and the executor keeps a catastrophe stop 3 R units away (eng-144, our deviation).; Checked loop-side               after each daily close (strategies.loopside.LoopDetectors._connors_daily). | strategies/classic.py:1 |

| DOC-0821 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Entry confirmations: independent evidence, measured at the entry, that the market backs the trade.; The trigger itself (a structure shift with agreeing taker flow, an accepted breakout, a confirmed phase) is the entry; these are the readings order-flow traders check before taking it, each recorded for every entry (journal `confirms`):  - flow       futures taker delta since the extreme (at most the last hour) agrees with the trade; - spot       spot taker delta over the same window agrees: real buying / selling of the coin, not only leverage              (spot-led moves hold, perp-led ones get squeezed); 'n/a' for a coin without a spot pair; - oi         open interest over the last hour (OI-weighted across venues when >= 2 report, else Binance): RISING for              continuations (trend, momentum, breakout: new positions back the move - price and OI together), FALLING              for sweeps and cascade exhaustion (the stops / liquidations were flushed); 'n/a' for Wyckoff (its phases              score OI themselves) and absorption (absorbed market orders open positions on both sides); - orderflow  at the extreme: stacked footprint imbalances, absorption (a heavy 1m delta against the trade that barely              moved price), a finished auction (the footprint's extreme price traded only by the side that was pushing:              nobody left to trade there, ATAS / Market Profile) or, for reversals, a CVD divergence (price made a new              extreme beyond the previous swing, cumulative delta did not: the aggressive side is exhausted); False              when none of them happened; - vsa        Volume Spread Analysis on the 5m bars since the extreme (strategies/vsa.py, Tom Williams): no supply /              stopping volume / reverse upthrust for longs, no demand / buying climax / upthrust for shorts.; Keys starting with '_' are metadata ('_window' = [since, until, extreme] ms, set by fetcher.confirmations), not readings.; Values: True / False, the order-flow kind (a string = present), NA ('n/a') where the reading cannot exist for this coin / strategy.; The inputs (1m candles of futures and spot, OI) are seeded from REST at start-up, so a reading is never missing after a restart; if one still is (a REST failure), it is None and never vetoes.; Which confirmations an entry needs: CONFIG.orderflow.required_confirmations (by rule: a breakout needs rising OI, like the D / F phases and directional impulses) plus those PROMOTED by the journal once entries without them show a negative Edge (journal.refresh_confirm_stats).; The rest are recorded and shown until stats.py proves them. | strategies/confirm.py:1 |

| DOC-0938 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | eng-106, Bollinger Method III exactly as John Bollinger's Method III manual (bollingerbands.com, 2021) states it:     "A tag of the lower Bollinger Band while Intraday Intensity is positive will create a buy alert ... a tag of the     upper Bollinger Band while Intraday Intensity is negative will generate a sell/short alert.; These are alerts rather     than signals ... wait for a strong up bar after a buy alert, or a strong down bar following a short alert".     - BB(20, 2); Intraday Intensity (Bostian) normalized over 21 bars: sum((2C - H - L) / (H - L) x V) / sum(V);     - the alert: a bar whose low tagged the lower band (high the upper) with II% > 0 (< 0), within the last 5 bars;     - the confirmation, on the bar that just closed (the first after the alert): a strong bar in the trade's way -       body >= half its range, close in the outer third, range >= the median range of the last 20 bars (ours: the       manual gives no number);     - stop beyond the extreme since the alert, the first target the opposite band (the manual names neither: ours, as       the W / M).; Journaled in shadow (SignalBook: 'bb3_shadow') beside the W / M. | strategies/maniac.py:512 |
| DOC-0939 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | John Bollinger's reversal method ("Bollinger on Bollinger Bands", 2001, method III; W / M patterns after Arthur     Merrill): a band tag is NOT a signal by itself.; W-bottom: a low that closed BELOW the lower band (%b < 0), a rally,     a retest low whose close stays INSIDE the band (%b >= 0, the price low may be lower), then the first close above the     rally's high between them -> long at that close; stop below the retest low; out at the OPPOSITE BAND as it moves     (Bollinger; eng-51 / 55), A/D must not confirm the retest.; M-top mirrored.; BB(20, 2), within the last 30 15m candles. | DOC-0958 | UNVERIFIED | OI build (eng-127): Martin Pring's open-interest rules (as summarized from his writing) - "prices rising and open interest increasing at a rate faster than its average = bullish", prices falling with open interest rising faster than its average = bearish (new positions behind the move).; A formula without our thresholds:  - at each 5m close, the 1h OI change (Binance, the 5-minute OI series of the last day) is above the MEAN of the   day's 1h OI changes and above zero, and the 1h price change has the trade's sign; - in at that close, the stop beyond the hour's extreme + the measured pierce, targets the opposite liquidity on the map.; No study shows OI changes predict crypto perpetual returns (and Binance's reported OI is known to be misquoted at times, arXiv 2310.14973), so the setup is journaled in SHADOW only ('oi_shadow': SignalBook) until its own rows show an edge.; Replaces the OI robots of eng-56 / eng-88 (cryptomillion's OI [15m] alert and our +5% / VWAP / ER floors). | strategies/oi_build.py:1 |

| DOC-0972 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | ICT's Power of Three / Judas swing (eng-58; innercircletrader.net, theinnercircletraders.com, ictkillzone.com - ICT publishes it only in videos, so the rule is the common ground of several write-ups): a day unfolds as accumulation (the Asian range), manipulation (a false move at the London open beyond one side of that range against the day's bias - the Judas swing, which takes the stops there) and distribution (the true move the other way).  - the Asian range: the high / low of 20:00-00:00 New York time (ICT's Asian range); - the window: the London kill zone, 02:00-05:00 New York time; - the Judas swing: a 5m bar inside the window trades beyond the Asian range's side OPPOSITE the trade (below the low   for a long); - the market structure shift: a displacement bar (body >= half its range) closes beyond the last 3-bar swing the raid   came from and leaves the first fair value gap after the raid; - the trade: a resting limit at the gap's CE, the stop beyond the raid's extreme (+ the measured pierce), targets the   Asian range's other side, then the day's draw (the previous day's high / low); - the day's bias is REQUIRED (weekly, else daily): without it there is no Judas swing to read ('no_bias').; Detected loop-side on the final 1m candles built into 5m bars (strategies.loopside.LoopDetectors._po3), once per coin, day and direction. | strategies/po3.py:1 |

| DOC-0996 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | Pumps (the owner's strategy, eng-39), built from published pieces, not invented thresholds:  - PUMP START (long): momentum ignition - a range expansion out of quiet trading (Crabel's volatility breakout / the   start of Wyckoff's markup) confirmed by participation: the last 15 minutes rose >= `pump_start_z` x the coin's own   15m volatility and >= `pump_start_min_pct`, on >= `pump_start_rvol` x the usual volume, with open interest rising   (new positions, not short covering) and spot taker buying (not a perp-only squeeze), price at a fresh 24h high.; Entry at market; stop below the low of the ignition (the quiet range the move left) + the measured pierce; targets   from the map (fillers in the move's own size). - PUMP TOP (short): the end of a pump - Wyckoff's buying climax / VSA's upthrust after a move of >= `pump_top_min_pct`   within 4h: a 15m candle at the high with a wick >= half its range on >= 2x the average volume (the climax), then the   5m structure shift down; open interest falling or the CVD diverging confirms.; Stop above the pump's high + the   measured pierce; targets the pump's 0.382 / 0.5 / 0.618 retracements (the mean of the leg) and the map. - Averaging 1:1 (the owner's rule): one more unit of the same size at half the way from the entry to the stop; the stop   stays; R is the sum of both units in the first unit's risk (SignalBook `add_at`).; Archive note (research/pumps_by_size.md): fading pumps of 60%+ was the worst setup (-0.19R); this short waits for the climax and the shift, it is not a fade of the rise itself.; Both are journaled and judged like every strategy. | strategies/pump.py:1 |

| DOC-1011 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | eng-104: ONE market regime per coin on the horizon of the trades (hours), replacing the eight scattered "trends" (1h EMA20, daily EMA50 + 30/90/365-day momentum, weekly EMA, 4h ADX, ICT daily / weekly closes ...).; The daily / weekly reads lagged by weeks: 2026-10-14 BTC was 'bull' by them while it fell for a week inside an 8.6% 20-day range.  - direction of a timeframe (1h, 4h): market structure - the last two confirmed swing highs AND lows (fractal 2) both   higher (+1) / both lower (-1) - agreeing with the EMA20 slope over 4 bars; else 0; - the range: the high / low of the last 20 CLOSED days; `pos` = where the price sits in it (0 low ..; 1 high); - regime:   'trend_up' / 'trend_down': 1h and 4h point the same way AND (ADX(14) of 4h >= 25 or the last 4h close is outside   the 20-day range - the breakout already happened);   'range': ADX(4h) < 25 and the price inside the 20-day range;   'mixed': anything else.; Shadow use (SignalBook._enter): a trend / breakout setup in a range pointing at the range's near edge ('rg_breakout_in_range': buying the top / selling the bottom of a range), any setup against a trend ('rg_countertrend').; Journaled in `regime2` and shadow_veto, nothing is blocked until the journal shows it. | strategies/regime.py:1 |

| DOC-1040 | UNVERIFIED | The Turtles' breakout systems as the original rules define them (Curtis Faith, "Way of the Turtle", 2007; "The Original Turtle Trading Rules"), on DAILY candles (fetcher._daily_candles_loop), checked loop-side every final minute (strategies.loopside.LoopDetectors._turtle_breakout):  - N = the 20-day exponential average of the true range: N = (19 x previous N + TR) / 20, seeded by the 20-day mean. - System 1: a trade the moment price exceeds the high (low) of the previous 20 days by a tick - intraday, on the touch,   not on a close.; Skipped when the LAST System 1 breakout (either direction, traded or not) would have been a winner. - System 2 (the fail-safe of a skipped System 1): the 55-day breakout, taken whatever the last one did. - Stop: 2N from the entry.; No profit target: System 1 exits on the 10-day low (a long) / high (a short), System 2 on   the 20-day one (Scanner / SignalBook trail the exit, `trail_days`). - Pyramiding (eng-34, SignalBook): +1 unit every 1/2 N from the previous unit's ACTUAL price (Faith), up to   turtle_max_units; the stop of all units moves to 2N from the newest one.; R stays the first unit's risk (2N), so a   full pyramid stopped out loses ~2-2.5R.; Not here: unit sizing (1% of equity per N). | strategies/turtle.py:1 |
| DOC-1041 | UNVERIFIED | Turtle N per day (aligned with d1; None before 21 days). | strategies/turtle.py:25 |
| DOC-1042 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | Replays System 1 over the daily history (one hypothetical position at a time, either direction).; Returns (state of the most recent S1 breakout, result of the last COMPLETED one before it):       'none'      - no S1 breakout in the history,       'open'      - its hypothetical trade is still open (no result yet),       'ambiguous' - its entry day also traded through the 2N stop: daily OHLC cannot tell which came first (open or                     closed, the result is unknown),       'win' / 'loss' - completed (a long that exited above its entry is a win).     audit: the N of a hypothetical entry is the one known BEFORE its day (ns[i - 1]); an open trade is never marked at     the last close. eng-137 (audit A04): the open ambiguous breakout was reported as the PREVIOUS completed result (or     none), which let a new S1 in while its own result was unknown. | strategies/turtle.py:39 |

| DOC-1046 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Volume Spread Analysis (Tom Williams, "Master the Markets"; Wyckoff's effort vs result): every bar read by its volume, its spread (high - low) and where it closed within the spread, against the bars before it.; For an entry the question is whether the bars since the extreme show the other side giving up: - long:  NO SUPPLY / TEST (a down bar with a narrow spread on volume below the two bars before: nobody sells),          STOPPING VOLUME (a down bar on >= 2x the average volume that closes in its upper half: buyers absorbed the          selling), SPRING-like bars count as tests; - short: NO DEMAND (an up bar, narrow spread, volume below the two before), UPTHRUST (a new high closing in its lower          third on volume above the two before), BUYING CLIMAX (an up bar on >= 2x volume closing in its lower half).; Bars against the trade with these signs of the OTHER side (no demand at a long, stopping volume at a short) make the reading negative.; Returns the sign's name (positive), False (an opposing sign or none) - a confirmation `vsa`. | strategies/vsa.py:1 |

| DOC-1050 | OVERSTATED_SCOPE | Wyckoff springs and upthrusts after distribution (UTAD) as Wyckoff defines them (Pruden, "The Three Skills of Top Trading"; Weis, "Trades About to Happen"): events of an established TRADING RANGE that followed a trend's stop, on 1h.  - The range is defined by Wyckoff's phase A EVENTS, not a bar count (eng-55; StockCharts' Wyckoff tutorial, Wyckoff   Analytics): the selling climax (SC: a wide 1h candle on climactic volume at a new 2-day low after a decline) sets   the range low, the automatic rally (AR: the high of the rebound after it) the range high, the secondary test (ST:   back toward the SC on LESS volume, holding above its low) completes phase A.; The decline it stopped: the close 48h   before the SC above the AR's high.; Distribution mirrored (BC -> AR -> ST, UTAD).; The quantification of "climactic"   (volume >= 2x and range >= 1.5x the 20 candles before) is ours: Wyckoff describes it, he gives no number. - SPRING (phase C): the 1h candle that just closed traded below the SC low and closed back inside, its lower wick >=   half the candle, the pierce <= 30% of the range height, after the ST and with the range unbroken (no close beyond   either edge since the ST).; UTAD mirrored at the AR / BC high. - The trade (Wyckoff buys the test, not the spring): a pullback toward the spring's area on less volume AND a narrower   spread (SignalBook: pullback_volume_ok / pullback_spread_ok); the entry on the 15m structure shift there; the stop   beyond the spring's low; targets: the range's far side, the point-and-figure count of the range (Wyckoff's cause ->   effect: columns x box x 3 from the spring's low; box = a tenth of the range height, 3-box reversal - our box   choice), then the map.; Replaces the 15m "BULL B / BEAR C" phase scores (a 5-hour window called a range; C was a CVD divergence at a new high with no range at all).; BULL E, the selling climax, is not bought: Wyckoff reads it as the range's start. | strategies/wyckoff_range.py:1 |
| DOC-1051 | UNVERIFIED | Wyckoff-owned cause/effect target ordering: far range, P&F proxy, then map. | strategies/wyckoff_range.py:32 |
| DOC-1052 | PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED | The latest SC -> AR -> ST (d = +1; BC -> AR -> ST for d = -1) before the last candle, with the range intact.     eng-120, formulas from Wyckoff's definitions (no thresholds of ours):     - the decline (rally) into the climax: from the last 2/2 swing high (low) that stands above (below) every price       after it, down to the climax;     - the selling climax (SC): the candle with the HIGHEST volume and the LOWEST low of that decline;     - the automatic rally (AR): the highest high after the SC;     - the secondary test (ST): the lowest low after the AR that holds above the SC's low on LESS volume than the SC;     - the prior trend: the decline started above the AR (price came from above the range);     searched over the whole history given (was: a week, a '2-day' low, a test within half the range - ours). | strategies/wyckoff_range.py:54 |
| DOC-1053 | UNVERIFIED | Columns of a close-only point-and-figure chart (box size `box`, `reversal`-box reversal). | strategies/wyckoff_range.py:117 |
| DOC-1054 | UNVERIFIED | Weis waves on closes: a wave runs until price closes `reversal` back from its extreme; (direction, volume). | strategies/wyckoff_range.py:140 |
| DOC-1055 | UNVERIFIED | Weis's effort into the spring (d = +1; the upthrust for -1): the volume of the last wave against the trade     divided by the mean of the earlier waves against it since the climax.; Below 1 = less effort into the test edge     (supply drying up, Weis "Trades About to Happen").; Journaled (`weis_ratio`), not a filter. | strategies/wyckoff_range.py:158 |

| DOC-1133 | UNVERIFIED | Level map of one coin: where price is likely to stall or where stops / liquidations sit, from several independent sources, merged into zones weighted by confluence. levels.py builds entry zones, stops and targets from it.; Sources (CONFIG.zones.weights): - swing_15m / swing_1h: fractal highs and lows (a high with `width` lower highs on each side); - equal: equal highs / lows (>= 2 swings within equal_tolerance_atr) that were not swept since: stop pools; - pdhl: previous UTC day's / week's high and low, the current day's high / low and the last completed sessions'   (Asia / London / New York, the exchanges' local hours in CONFIG.strategies.sweep_sessions) highs / lows: where stops rest; - book: order-book liquidity that stayed (heatmap bands, depth.py); - poc / va / hvn: volume profile of 5m candles over ~4 days and of 15m candles over ~24h, each candle's volume spread evenly over its   range: point of control, value-area edges (CBOT method), high-volume nodes; - liq_est: ESTIMATED liquidation clusters: every 1h OI increase at price P opened as many longs as shorts (each   contract has both sides); each side is spread over leverage tiers, the aggressive (taker) side of the candle is   assumed to use more leverage (its weight shifts towards the high tiers); a position at leverage L is liquidated around   P * (1 -+ 1/L +- maintenance).; OI decreases remove positions proportionally; clusters price already crossed are   gone.; A model (like the public liquidation heatmaps), not data; - liq_hit: prices where liquidations were actually observed in the last 24h (a pool already swept); - wall: persistent order-book walls (hot coins only, depth.py); - liq_hl: REAL liquidation prices of large Hyperliquid accounts (positioning.py), summed per price bucket; - vwap: VWAP of the current UTC day and VWAP anchored at the measured leg's start; - fib / range: Fibonacci levels of a leg and a trading range's edges (added by the strategies' plans).; Everything is a hypothesis: the journal stores which sources each target and stop came from, and stats.py measures how often each kind was reached. | zones.py:1 |
